#2786. Rams make change again at special teams, release Alex Bachman

The Los Angeles Rams have decided to move on from Alex Bachman, waiving the special teams returner as the team looks for a more dynamic option in the return game. Bachman's limitations in acceleration, top-end speed and elusiveness ultimately made it difficult for him to establish himself as a reliable long-term solution.

Bachman had an opportunity to carve out a role with the Rams by contributing on special teams, but the return game demands more than simply securing the football. Bachman failed to hit openings quickly, make defenders miss in space, and turn promising blocking into explosive plays.

For a Rams team looking to improve its special teams production, the decision to move on reflects the importance of finding a returner capable of changing field position. Los Angeles has struggled to consistently generate positive results in that phase, making the search for a more explosive option an important roster consideration.

The Rams could look internally or explore the available market to find their next return specialist.

Internal options: The coaching staff could turn to players already on the roster who have experience handling kickoffs or punts. Tutu Atwell's speed makes him an intriguing possibility if the team wants to explore a more explosive option, although his past track record did not appear to yield much either.

External options: Los Angeles could examine available free agents, recently released players or return specialists on other teams' practice squads. Josh Downs of the Indianapolis Colts has return experience, quick acceleration and the ability to make defenders miss. Downs also provides a role as a depth wide receiver option as the Rams continue to mull WR3 options.

#2782. 3 Candidates to Replace Colts Head Coach Shane Steichen With Firing Imminent

It has been a pretty brutal season for the Indianapolis Colts. The team is 2-6 on the season and just got blown out by the Jacksonville Jaguars after trading away some of its best players. General manager Chris Ballard has already been fired and more players could potentially be traded away so it feels like Steichen's time with the Colts is going to come to an end fairly soon.

Whether it comes during the season or once the regular season has concluded, it is just hard to see Steichen keeping his job unless the Colts have a huge turnaround and prove that they want to keep playing for their head coach. That will be difficult though especially if the Colts continue to trade away their best players. Jonathan Taylor and Josh Downs could be the next players on the move.

If the Colts do get rid of Steichen during the season then there will surely be an internal replacement on the current staff, unless they opt for another Jeff Saturday experiment, but it is not a bad idea to think ahead about who might be a potential replacement in the offseason.

If the Colts want to bring in an experienced coach looking for a new challenge then they wouldn't be able to do any better than Mike Tomlin. He never had a losing season with the Pittsburgh Steelers and maybe he could come in and turn the Colts around similar to how John Harbaugh has gotten better football out of the New York Giants.

Like Tomlin, McDermott comes from a long-term stint with one organization. He led the Buffalo Bills for years and was let go because he was unable to get them over the hump but it is inarguable that he was able to create a winning culture there so perhaps he could replicate the same thing with the Colts.

It seems like a lot of NFL coaching hires in recent years have been centered on guys with familiar last names. The Arizona Cardinals hired Mike LaFleur, brother of Green Bay Packers head coach Matt LaFleur, as their head coach and the Las Vegas Raiders hired Klint Kubiak, son of Gary Kubiak, to be their head coach.

If Kubiak's are getting hired then the next choice should be obvious: San Francisco 49ers offensive coordinator Klay Kubiak. He has studied under head coach Kyle Shanahan and it seems like a lot of newly hired NFL coaches have some connection to the Shanahan coaching tree so Kubiak could be a fit.

The Colts are hitting the reset button so they need to find someone who can help build the franchise back up. It will not be easy but perhaps one of these coaches can get things turned around in Indy.

#2778. Colts reportedly extend promising DT Adetomiwa Adebawore amid roster purge

The Indianapolis Colts aren't purging their entire roster.

Per NFL Network, the Colts and defensive lineman Adetomiwa Adebawore have agreed to a $35 million contract extension with a $13 million roster bonus.

The deal adds $22.5 million in new money to his contract through the 2027 season, per the report. A fourth-round selection in the 2023 NFL Draft, Adebawore was previously on track to became a free agent in the offseason at the conclusion of his four-year, $4.6 million rookie contract.

Adebawore, 25, has tied his career high with four sacks through eight games in addition to recording 19 tackles and eight quarterback hits. He's emerged as one of the Colts' more promising young players.

Adetomiwa Adebawore has a $35 million contract extension after playing on a four-year, $4.6 million rookie deal.

Adebawore was the subject of trade speculation amid an ongoing roster purge and rebuild in the aftermath of the dismissal of longtime general manager Chris Ballard on Oct. 19. A Colts team that invested in quarterback Daniel Jones via a two-year, $88 million extension in the offseason has since begun to offload its top veteran players amid a 2-6 start to the season.

The Colts traded perennial All-Pro guard Quenton Nelson to the Seahawks on Oct. 27, then dealt former All-Pro defensive tackle DeForest Buckner to the 49ers hours later. The trades netted the Colts third- and fourth-round picks in 2027 and a fifth-round selection in 2028 as they set their sights squarely on building for the future.

Three-time Pro Bowl running back and fan favorite Jonathan Taylor is reportedly among the players who's also on the trade block ahead of the NFL's Nov. 10 trade deadline. Starting wide receiver Josh Downs has requested a trade.

Adebawore was also believed to be on the block, but the Colts have signaled their intent to keep him by extending his contract. Adebawore can conceivably contribute to a Colts turnaround if they choose to keep him beyond his extension. Taylor, 27, Nelson, 30, and Buckner, 32, are better suited to help teams that are ready to win now.

#2777. Giannis Antetokounmpo had very high praise for Luka Dončić after Lakers win over Heat

In what has become commonplace this season, even for his illustrious standards, Luka Dončić authored a magnificent performance during Monday night's 138-117 rout of the Miami Heat. The Slovenian superstar recorded 42 points (14-of-24 shooting), 13 assists (two turnovers), seven rebounds and two steals. After a couple relatively quiet games to begin the year, Dončić is averaging 43.4 points, 8.6 assists (3.6 turnovers), 8.0 rebounds and 2.0 steals over his past five outings.

"It feels good. Like I say, obviously when we win, it feels way better and that's the only important stuff," Dončić told reporters postgame. "But, obviously, it feels good. When you win by 20 against a team like Miami, it's really awesome, especially on a back to back."

While the 2-5 Heat are struggling out of the gates and the Toronto Raptors were without Kawhi Leonard, the Sunday-Monday back-to-back this week projected as one of Los Angeles' tougher early season tests when the schedule came out months ago. Of course, circumstances change but a pair of convincing wins (Sunday's late-game foibles aside) against two playoff hopefuls is a nice way for the Lakers to kick off November.

Following the Heat's loss, Giannis Antetokounmpo was asked by a reporter about how Dončić thrived as both a playmaker and scorer, given the fact defenses typically take away one of those aspects for a superstar while conceding the other. Antetokounmpo used the inquiry to bestow lofty praise upon Dončić, not just for his current league-wide standing but where he stacks up among the all-time greats.

"When you have a guy like Luka, obviously he's one of the best scorers to ever play this game, if not the best," Antetokounmpo said.

When the reporter offered a "right now" caveat to Antetokounmpo's statement, the two-time MVP pushed back.

"Not right now, not right now. Of all time," he said. "I feel like some people are scared to say that but it's gotta be up there."

Dončić is on an absolute tear right now, leading the Lakers' second-ranked offense and playing some of the stingiest defense of his career. His next chance to keep the good times rolling comes Wednesday night on the road against the Sacramento Kings at 7 p.m.

#2775. A Successful Mess: Marquette 80, Mississippi Valley State 42

The longest run of the game for YOUR Marquette Golden Eagles women's basketball game in their season opener on Monday night was the only one they needed to take control of the game. After buckets for both sides in the opening two minutes, Halle Vice converted a layup to kick off an 11-0 run by the Golden Eagles. 13-2 Marquette, and that was, effectively, that.

Yes, MU was only up 17-8 at the end of the first quarter after an and-1 for I'Xana Winters with four seconds left, so there was some mystery left in the bag. Kayl Petersen and Vice opened up the scoring in the second quarter to put Marquette up 13, and the lead was never in single digits again. A triple from Petersen with 1:48 left before the half moved the margin to 20 for the first time, and Petersen hit a fadeaway at the horn to make it 26, 39-13, at intermission.

MacKenzie Givens got the bucket to get the lead to 30 for the first time, scoring in transition to make it 46-15 with 8:17 left in the third. The biggest margin of that quarter was 35, and some late scoring from the Golden Eagles got the differential to its peak of 38, which is where it ended at 80-42.

The closest this got to drama after those Petersen and Vice buckets in the second quarter was when MVSU scored back to back buckets in the first 45 seconds of the fourth quarter. MU head coach Cara Consuegra was FURIOUS at what had transpired to lead to the two buckets, called timeout, and audibly hollered "GET OVER HERE. SIT DOWN." at the five women on the floor. None of the five were Halle Vice or Jordan Meulemans, which is probably part of the problem for the Golden Eagles. Those are the only two returning rotation players for Marquette this year with everyone else coming in as 1) a little used bench option last year, 2) a transfer, 3) a freshman, or 4) coming off an injury last year and not dressing to play here. There were always going to be rough patches as this team comes together, and this was one of them. In this case, all it did was drift the lead under 30 points.

By the way: Marquette fumbled the ensuing inbounds after the timeout. Ever seen a college basketball coach literally facepalm during a game? I have.

The good news on the day: Halle Vice was awesome, as hoped for/expected. 17 points, 16 rebounds, three assists, a block, and two steals in just 24 minutes, and who knows what she would have done if she played more towards 30 minutes in a slightly more competitive game.

The bad news on the day: 25 turnovers for the team, which works out to 31% of Marquette's possessions in the game. None of this mattered while the Golden Eagles shot 36% on three-pointers and 51% on twos. None of the MU turnovers mattered while MVSU shot 21.2% from the field for the game. But against a different team on a different day (glances at Saturday's opponent), maybe it does.

Good performances in debuts: Kinley Asp, Kayl Petersen, and MacKenzie Givens all started for Marquette in their first games here. Asp had six rebounds and five assists, although she had five of MU's turnovers, too. Petersen had 10 points, five rebounds, three assists, and two blocks. Givens went for a not great 14 points because she was just 5-for-11 from the field. I felt like she was struggling with her shot for a while in the first half, and then I realized she had seven points and that's fine then. Yu Han Lin was MU's last double digit scorer, getting 14 in her Marquette debut on 4-for-8 shooting, including 2-for-5 (40%!) behind the three-point line. She also had five turnovers, which we noted as a problem during her year at Middle Tennessee, and she was called for at least one completely atrociously obvious offensive foul for a push off in transition with the defender well in front of her trying to get back. 1) It was an obvious foul, 2) Lin complained about it to the referee. I think I may have identified her turnover problem from last year. Amina Traore came off the bench for 20 minutes, and went 1-for-4 from the field, which is not great…. but her 11 rebounds are AWESOME, same for her two assists and the steal as well.

#2774. Seven players have 2027-28 rookie contract options declined

Missing in the first round of an NBA Draft is a setback in team building, but for seven teams this year it was better to accept the pain now rather than keep the player around for the final year of their rookie deal.

Seven players did not have their rookie-scale team options for the 2027-28 season picked up, the deadline was Monday. Those players are (via Keith Smith of Spotrac), in order of most to least expensive deals declined:

• Tidjane Salaun, Charlotte Hornets ($10.4 million)• Cody Williams, Minnesota Timberwolves ($7.6 million)• Nikola Topic, Oklahoma City Thunder ($7.5 million)• A.J. Johnson, New Orleans Pelicans ($5.5 million)• DaRon Holmes II, Denver Nuggets ($5.5 million)• Pacome Dadiet, New York Knicks ($5.4 million)• Isaiah Collier, Utah Jazz($4.8 million)

Some of these were simply financial decisions in the apron era, such as Pacome Dadiet with the Knicks. Three players were outright waived by their teams before this season:

• Rob Dillingham (waived by the Hornets after being traded for Buddy Hield, has yet to re-sign)• Dalton Knecht (waived by the Lakers, signed as a two-way player with the Knicks)• Devin Carter (waived by Hawks, signed as a two-way player by Celtics)

There were 19 players who had their extensions picked up, they are extension-eligible next summer or could be headed to 2028 restricted free agency. #2773. This time is different for the Chicago Bears

The Chicago Bears' blockbuster trade for Jeffery Simmons drew a lot of comparisons to their 2018 blockbuster trade for Khalil Mack. Both defenders were acquired to give a team with Super Bowl aspirations a boost, but the big difference is that this time the franchise knows exactly who they are with Ben Johnson and Caleb Williams, while the 2018 team was headed into year one of Matt Nagy and year two of Mitchell Trubisky.

There wasn't proof of concept showing that Nagy/Mitch was the right pairing to lead the franchise to a championship. It was just hope. They hoped Trubisky would take a year-two leap. They hoped Nagy was the right guy for the job. They hoped the new look offense would gel and complement Vic Fangio's defense.

Whereas the Ben/Caleb pairing had a full year to cook, and part of 2026 to show improvement, which they did. This team was already firmly in the postseason mix thanks to the offense, so adding a game-wrecker to a well-coached Dennis Allen defense was a calculated gamble that made perfect sense.

The Bears have a head coach/quarterback combo who can lead them to greatness and be mentioned in the same breath as Walsh/Montana and Reid/Mahomes.

The nerds will try to use their spreadsheets to prove something about sustainability, regression, or some other dumb thing that can't be measured. Analysts will point to history as to why this or that can't happen. The haters will still try to poke holes no matter what their eyes tell them.

But this time it's different, and they know it.

#2772. Kyler Murray's hamstring issue could be cover for a soft benching

How can the Vikings bench Kyler Murray without benching Kyler Murray?

They can rely on an injury through which he otherwise would be able to play.

It’s hard not to look at the current status of Murray’s hamstring “tightness” as cover for a soft benching of Murray. That gives the Vikings a chance to let Carson Wentz, who played well enough in most of the Week 1 win over the Packers and all of the Week 2 win over the Bears.

While the Vikings will never admit to that strategy, consider this undeniable fact: On Tuesday, offensive coordinator Wes Phillips was asked for an assessment of Murray’s play “in the past couple weeks.”

“I don’t really want to get into, like, overarching, ‘Hey, this is this is the . . .,” Phillips said. “You know, the fact of the matter is, you know, we lost the game against a tough [Lions] football team on the road. It’s a division game. It stings, but we’ve got to get better moving forward.  And, you know, we’ll see as the week goes on, where we’re at in that spot . . . but, yeah, we’ve got to get better. We’re going to actively pursue every avenue we can to make that happen.”

This implies, if taken literally, pursuing every avenue toward acquiring an upgrade via a trade — whether that’s Jacoby Brissett, Daniel Jones, Kirk Cousins, or even Justin Herbert.

It all comes down to whether the Vikings are content to keep doing what they’ve been doing, or whether they’ll make a move aimed at significantly upgrading a position that, in recent weeks, has not been good enough.

Yes, they’re 6-1. That’s better than anyone could have expected them to be. But it’s not because Murray has turned the clock back to the early years of his career. His passer rating (86.6) is toward the bottom of all qualifying quarterbacks (and, in fairness, that’s higher than the numbers generated by Brissett, Jones, and Herbert).

It’s the lowest number for any season of Murray’s career, and the overall performance is less than what the Vikings need to maximize their current formula.

Especially since he’s not getting the ball to receiver Justin Jefferson. That’s something Wentz did consistently in 2025, making Wentz the preferred option to J.J. McCarthy — who returned to the lineup following a Week 2 ankle injury only after Wentz suffered a season-ending shoulder injury in Week 8.

If McCarthy was still in Minnesota, there would be an unavoidable groundswell to give him a shot. But he lost the No. 2 job to Wentz. Which means Wentz won it.

Which means that the Vikings could be thinking about giving Wentz a Monday night audition against the Bills, with a plan to make a possible trade the next day if things don’t go well.

For now, the rest of the week will reveal whether and to what extent Murray or Wentz will take the first-team reps at practice. And whether Murray or Wentz will play on Monday night.

It won’t be a surprise if it’s Wentz against the Bills. Even if Murray is technically healthy enough to play.

#2771. Fun, flowing, not yet flawless: Lakers' league-best offense still has room to improve

Austin Reaves described it as "fun." Luka Doncic called the Lakers' effortless-looking offense as "flowing." And this is still the early stages of development for a team that brought in nine new players.

Through eight games, the Lakers have the most dynamic offense in the NBA. Led by Doncic, the Lakers are second in scoring with 122.5 points per game and first in offensive rating with 121.9 points per 100 possessions. The starting lineup of Doncic, Reaves, Walker Kessler, Ziaire Williams and Matisse Thybulle has a league-best 135.5 offensive rating among five-man groups that have played at least 40 minutes together entering Wednesday's game at Sacramento.

"Everything's as expected," guard Quentin Grimes said Monday after the Lakers hung 138 points in a 21-point win over the Miami Heat. "Winning, everybody's getting involved, everybody's making the right play, getting a lot of assists."

The Lakers (6-2) had 39 assists against the Heat, the most during JJ Redick's head-coaching tenure. Doncic is on a historic scoring binge; he is the first Laker since Kobe Bryant in 2007 to have five consecutive games of 35 or more points. While putting up a seemingly ho-hum 42 points against Miami, he also had a season-high 13 assists.

"It's a really good offense that we're playing right now," Redick said. "And a lot of that, of course, is Doncic and what he's able to do and bend the defense."

Doncic, the NBA's leading scorer at 38.3 points per game, does his damage early. He typically plays all 12 minutes of the opening quarter and leads the league with 15 points a game in the first quarter. Once he gets his shot going, teams send double teams to force the ball out of his hands. That's when Doncic can be particularly dangerous.

Read more: Luka Doncic's 42-point effort carries Lakers past Giannis Antetokounmpo and Heat

Doncic has always been lauded for his superior court vision. But the Lakers are still learning to see the same picture. The Lakers had 14 turnovers, including 11 from Doncic and Reaves, during a preseason game against the Denver Nuggets, who were aggressively trapping the Lakers' ballhandlers. The Lakers threw away a 25-point second-quarter lead on Oct. 30 when the Golden State Warriors ratcheted up the ball pressure.

Doncic's five turnovers a game are the second most in the league, and Reaves is third with 4.6 turnovers per game.

Some players are new to being on a team with guards who get blitzed nearly every play, Reaves said. It requires players to not only be comfortable making decisions to pass, drive or shoot, but they have to choose quickly. A moment's hesitation gives away the four-on-three advantage created by the double-team.

Redick praised Grimes' ability to keep advantages by driving decisively. Forward Sandro Mamukelashvili is also great at it, Redick said, as is guard Collin Sexton, who knows no speed other than full. The Lakers have also finished their advantages with Thybulle and Williams making27 of 58 (46.6%) three-point attempts combined.

"That's the beauty of our team," Williams said. "You could put different guys at different spots and everyone's able to make a play. All our guys are just dynamic and versatile. And everyone just wants to make the right play. No one wants to only shoot, there's no egos. And that's the fun part about it, you know? We all love to see each other succeed."

Williams missed three games because of a sore left foot but returned Monday with nine points, two rebounds and two assists off the bench. When Jake LaRavia jumped into the starting lineup, the Lakers were just as effective offensively as their first-choice lineup. When Doncic, Reaves, Kessler, Thybulle and LaRavia are together, the Lakers' 144.3 points per 100 possessions ranks first among lineups that have played at least 30 minutes.

Heat forward Giannis Antetokounmpo reaches through the hoop to block a reverse dunk by Lakers center Walker Kessler on Monday night. (Robert Gauthier / Los Angeles Times)

But numbers don't normalize until about 250 minutes, Redick believes.

"I think there's always room for improvement," he said Monday.

The Lakers have the most room for improvement on the defensive side. Their defensive rating of 114 points allowed per 100 possessions is tied with the Clippers for 20th in the NBA. They still rank 28th in rebounding, despite three consecutive double-doubles from Kessler, who responded after Redick challenged the center to be a larger rebounding presence.

"This team can become a great team," Redick said before Monday's win over the Heat. "That requires patience, that requires a lot of coaching and teaching and buy-in, and so that whole process takes time."

Sign up for our weekly newsletter on all things Lakers.

This story originally appeared in Los Angeles Times.

#2770. National Title Winning RB Blasts CFP Rankings After Texas Lands Higher Than UCLA

The UCLA Bruins sit at No. 5 in both the AP and Coaches Poll. But landed at No. 8 in the College Football Playoff rankings unveiled Tuesday, even falling behind two-loss Texas. And that brought out an agitated UCLA backer in ex-Alabama running back Damien Harris.

The CBS Sports college football analyst and former New England Patriots back went scorched earth in calling out the CFP rankings.

Harris joined the rest of the panel in breaking down the first CFP rankings. And immediately zeroed in on how the Bruins deserve to be higher than the Longhorns.

"Does UCLA have more wins than Texas? They DO!" Harris shouted out. "Everybody in their right mind knows that UCLA with [coach] Bob Chesney, [running back] Wayne Knight and this defense that's second in takeaways…they are better than the Texas Longhorns."

Harris wasn't through, asking "What does Texas have to hang their hat on?"

He even added this statement that will grab the attention of folks in Austin and the Texas campus.

"That Ohio State win is no longer impressive," Harris said, as the Buckeyes also own two losses like the 'Horns.

So the Bruins are higher ranked in the other two prominent rankings. That includes landing higher compared to the likes of defending national champion Indiana, or 7-2 Big Ten team and annual rival USC (Trojans earned the 20th ranking in the CFP).

But it's IU, not UCLA, who sits higher at No. 5 thanks to the Tuesday announcement.

Indiana still owns one loss, which came to the aforementioned two-loss Buckeyes. But the Hoosiers aren't the only team that somehow catapulted the Bruins.

Alabama joins Texas as two-loss teams earning a better spot compared to UCLA, with the Crimson Tide at No. 5. The Crimson Tide fell to No. 2 Georgia but also No. 14 Tennessee (which took 13th in the playoff rankings).

Kentucky is the only other team with a loss that owns a better CFP spot compared to the Bruins by earning the sixth slot. But UCLA is the only unbeaten falling outside of the top five of the CFP.

Now Chesney and the Bruins have their potential trap game coming up.

A red-hot Minnesota team is up next on Saturday in TCF Bank Stadium. The Golden Gophers own one top 25 victory already in toppling No. 21 Iowa at the same venue. Plus took the Hoosiers to the wire one week ago before Indiana pulled away 23-16.

So the Gophers' venue will be rocking as their team aims to stun the college football world. And potentially make the CFP committee look like geniuses for ranking UCLA lower.

But the Bruins control their own destiny too in their path to the Big Ten Championship game. UCLA winning out places the Bruins in Indianapolis on Dec. 5 with the conference crown on the line. Which in turn seals the Bruins' playoff fate as one of the Big Ten representatives in the field of 12.

Savannah James attends the Blue Jacket Fashion Show, an event supporting prostate cancer awareness and men's health through fashion and philanthropy, in Los Angeles, Wednesday, Sept. 30, 2026. BFA.com / BACKGRID

The James family may not reside together anymore, but Savannah appears determined to be the glue of the group.

#2765. Bayern frustrated, Stuttgart bounce back and Elversberg run riot – 5 things we learned from Bundesliga Matchday 8

Eight matches into the Bundesliga season, Bayern Munich and Borussia Dortmund remain unbeaten at the top of the league, locked on 18 points after their showdown at the Allianz Arena.

Elsewhere, Freiburg gained ground on the top two, Werder Bremen's run continued, and Stuttgart finally showed signs of life on their trip to Leverkusen.

Looking back at the weekend, here are the five biggest talking points from Matchday 8.

On an evening when Harry Kane was honoured for winning the Ballon d’Or, the Englishman endured a rare frustrating night as Bayern Munich were held to a draw by Borussia Dortmund.

Kane struck the crossbar from the penalty spot, as Bayern hit the woodwork four times and twice let their lead slip away.

Dortmund, however, deserve credit for their persistence.

After struggling for attacking creativity in recent weeks, Niko Kovac’s side showed far more ambition in Munich, with goals from Serhou Guirassy and Fabio Silva sealing a point on their travels.

However, Bayern will feel they should have claimed all three points, with the side coached by Aaron Danks producing an xG of 4.48 compared to Dortmund's 0.74 of Dortmund, while Lennart Karl came close to netting a winner in the dying moments as his effort from distance struck the bar.

Then, there may also be bigger concerns in the treatment room, with Ismael Saibari, Serge Gnabry, and Michael Olise all forced off through injury ahead of Tuesday's trip to Atletico Madrid - a more than difficult place to go with a thin squad.

With boss Vincent Kompany still recovering and Bayern looking increasingly light across the squad, Tuesday's clash could be a real banana skin, but it could also be a real statement as the international break nears.

A week ago, Stuttgart were sitting 15th after suffering an embarrassing home defeat to a Gladbach side that had previously lost every Bundesliga match.

Saturday’s emphatic victory at Bayer Leverkusen was quite the response.

Ermedin Demirovic struck twice, with Jamie Leweling and Bilal El Khannouss also on target as Sebastian Hoeneß’s side produced comfortably their best attacking performance of the campaign.

It was a result that VfB had been threatening since the start of the campaign, having created bucketloads of chances in recent defeats, only to end up lacking the edge in the final third.

However, Hoeneß's side's xG sat at 3.23 on Saturday afternoon, and for once they overperformed rather than underperformed, while skipper Deniz Undav also played an important part, creating Demirovic's second goal, and Dennis Seimen impressed on his return between the posts.

Having lost five of their opening seven matches, Stuttgart desperately needed a result of this magnitude.

They remain in the bottom half, but Saturday offered a glimpse of the side that supporters have become accustomed to watching under Hoeneß.

The challenge now is producing that on a consistent basis, with the midweek clash with Galatasaray to come next.

It is becoming increasingly difficult to describe Elversberg’s results as surprises.

The Bundesliga newcomers recorded their fourth victory in eight matches on Friday evening, putting four past Mainz in another thoroughly entertaining performance.

Felix Keidel’s excellent free-kick set Vincent Wagner’s side on their way, before Francis Onyeka and Maurice Krattenmacher established a three-goal advantage before half-time.

Cole Campbell then provided the highlight of the evening with a spectacular acrobatic finish in stoppage time.

With 13 points already collected, the Saarland club are comfortably holding their own in their first Bundesliga season.

Mainz, meanwhile, have now suffered three consecutive defeats in all competitions and were particularly poor during the opening 45 minutes.

Nobody at Elversberg will be getting carried away after eight matches, but their early results suggest they have every reason to believe they belong at this level.

After consecutive league defeats to Leverkusen and Bayern, Freiburg looked set for another disappointing afternoon when they trailed Augsburg at half-time on Saturday.

However, Julian Schuster’s substitutions completely changed the contest.

Igor Matanovic levelled the score after being introduced at the break, before fellow substitute Cyriaque Irie completed the comeback five minutes from time.

Remarkably, all four players directly involved in Freiburg’s two goals had started the afternoon on the bench, with Keisuke Goto and Vincenzo Grifo providing the assists.

“We told the lads last night that there was a good chance their moment would come today,” Schuster explained afterwards.

Freiburg were far from their best, with Augsburg enjoying several promising opportunities, but the visitors took theirs when it mattered.

The victory leaves Freiburg third on 16 points, just two behind Bayern and Dortmund.

The goals are not flowing as they were at the start of the season, but the Black Forest club are more than capable of grinding out wins when called upon, as seen in the cup against Wolfsburg last week, and against Augsburg too.

With a clash against Union Berlin to come next weekend, there is every chance that Freiburg go into the international break sitting in the Champions League spots - fully deserved after an excellent start to the term.

Niclas Füllkrug’s goalscoring exploits have dominated the conversation around Werder Bremen in recent weeks, and understandably so.

The 33-year-old continued his remarkable run against Hoffenheim on Saturday, scoring for the seventh consecutive Bundesliga match to take his season tally to nine.

However, Daniel Thioune will have been equally pleased with what happened at the other end.

Werder recorded a clean sheet against a Hoffenheim side that had scored three against Bayer Leverkusen the previous weekend, with Karl Hein impressing between the sticks, before Eren Dinkci sealed the victory with a superb finish late on.

As a result, Bremen's unbeaten Bundesliga run now sits at seven matches, leaving Die Grün-Weißen sitting fifth in the standings just half a year on from battling to stay in the division.

There was some concern surrounding Mitchell Weiser, who departed with an apparent muscular injury just weeks after returning from a lengthy absence, but with Füllkrug scoring regularly and the backline improving, Thioune has plenty to be pleased about.

Bremen have put 15 points on the board in eight outings, and like Freiburg, could well find themselves in the top four over the international break if they seal a strong result in Stuttgart next week.

#2764. Cops complain about LeBron James during J.R. Smith assault arrest: ‘I’m still mad’

Bodycamera video shows J.R. Smith talking with police officers in Texas. ROYSE CITY PD

J.R. Smith chats with police officers about the Lakers in September. ROYSE CITY PD

Cops stopped J.R. Smith after they suspected he had a warrant out for his arrest. ROYSE CITY PD

JR Smith of the Los Angeles Lakers drives to the basket against the Toronto Raptors on Saturday, Aug. 1, 2020, at The Arena in Orlando, Fla. NBAE via Getty Images

"Nobody was happy about that," one of the other cops on the scene added.

Los Angeles Lakers forward LeBron James controls the ball against New York Knicks guard Landry Shamet during a game Sunday, Feb. 1, 2026, in New York. Brad Penner-Imagn Images

It was eventually revealed that Smith was wanted by the Southlake Police Department for assault causing bodily injury — family violence after he allegedly got physical with a woman during a Sept. 18 incident in Tarrant County.

Smith was taken into custody over the allegations and released a short time later. His attorney, Justin A. Moore, said in a statement after the run-in with cops that his client was "eager to see this matter resolved and has full confidence in the legal process."

#2762. Rashan Gary’s inexcusable penalty opened the door to final collapse

The Cowboys should have had Arizona facing a desperate decision on a fourth down. Either go for a risky first down on 4th-and-10, or take a safe field goal that would still leave Dallas up seven. Instead, Rashan Gary handed them a lifeline.

With Dallas leading 27-17 late in the fourth quarter, Gary's roughing-the-passer penalty wiped out an incompletion and gave the Cardinals a fresh set of downs. One play later, Arizona was in the end zone. Suddenly, a game the Cowboys should have closed out became another nervous finish, ultimately ending in a 33-30 overtime defeat.

Dallas had already weathered one Arizona response. After taking a commanding 24-10 lead early in the fourth quarter, the Cowboys allowed Tyler Allgeier to score again, cutting the margin to just seven. However, the Cowboys' offense then put together a lengthy possession, before Brandon Aubrey's 35-yard field goal upped the lead to 27-17 with 3:54 remaining. With the clock working in their favor, Dallas should have held on to the victory.

Dallas needed the defense to make Arizona earn every play. Instead, on third-and-10 from the Dallas 29 with 2:23 remaining, Gardner Minshew's pass towards the end zone fell incomplete. However, Gary committed an unnecessary helmet-to-helmet hit on the quarterback, giving Arizona 15 yards and an automatic first down at the Dallas 14. This turned fourth-and-10 into another scoring opportunity for Arizona without the Cardinals having to complete a pass.

It was an inexcusable mistake from a veteran defender. The punishment was immediate. Minshew found an uncovered Trey McBride for a 14-yard touchdown on the next snap. The extra point cut Dallas' lead to 27-24, and Arizona had scored before the two-minute warning.

The timing of Gary's mistake made the penalty even more damaging. Arizona still had the two-minute warning to stop the clock during Dallas' next possession. Gary's error had helped the Cardinals quickly get into the end zone, preserving a realistic chance of regaining possession. This desperate comeback now had a viable path forward.

Gary does not carry all the blame for what followed. The coverage failure on McBride's touchdown, the offense's inability to close and the final defensive collapse each deserves scrutiny. But his penalty was the moment Dallas surrendered a major advantage it had already earned. With Arizona running short of chances, the Cowboys supplied another one for free. For a team trying to prove it could win consecutive games, that lack of discipline was painfully costly, and this was the turning point of the game.

#2761. Player Grades: Knicks 103, Nets 90

For three quarters Monday night, the Knicks looked like they might actually lose to the Nets. The Nets? The last place Nets? While we might get apoplectic, the champs seemed unconcerned about falling behind. When they finally flipped the switch, they turned a 79–75 deficit into a 103–90 win, outscoring Brooklyn 28–11 in the fourth at the Garden. That's 15 straight wins against the neighbors. Landry Shamet did a lot of the work to make it so.

22 points (6–8 FG, 4–6 3PT, 6–6 FT), 1 rebound, 1 assist in 30 minutes

On a night when the starters happily hurled threes like pre-teens playing Pop Shot, Shamet sank a steady four of six, plus every free throw he took. His season-high 22 kept the Knicks in it long enough for the rest of them to wake up.

While the Knicks spun their wheels in third-quarter mud, Josh Hart collected his fourth foul with a lotta game remaining. Shamet replaced him at 8:44 and scored six points on perfect shooting from downtown. The first, at 2:26, cut Brooklyn's lead to a single point; the second, with a minute left, made it 77-75. According to Basketball Reference, he had a team-high game score of 19.4. Pretty good for a sub.

8 points (3–6 FG, 2–5 3PT), 6 rebounds, 7 assists, 3 steals in 27 minutes (+22)

Hart had a crooked finger in just about everything that made the fourth quarter exciting. In that final frame, he hit a Kolek-assisted three to pull the Knicks within one, then assisted on back-to-back Anunoby threes and a Kolek layup (everybody seems to enjoy playing with Tyler, don't they?). Josh intercepted a Julius Randle pass and took it the other way for a layup. His grade suffers for taking just six shots and collecting six fouls, but maybe he deserves a little more love for being a team high +22?

18 points (4–10 FG, 3–7 3PT, 7–8 FT), 4 rebounds, 2 assists, 2 steals in 38 minutes

OG played 38 minutes, got to the line eight times, and hit back-to-back threes in the fourth when the Knicks finally started to put Brooklyn away. Seven free throws will do a lot for a night when only four shots fall from the floor. Give credit to his stamina: after bumping bodies with that bull Julius Randle all night, he still had gas in the tank to help his team in the fourth. He hit the go-ahead three at 10:29 and another bomb 28 seconds later to make it 84–79. He also set up Clarkson's transition three. Loses points for still struggling to finish at the rim.

11 points (4–11 FG, 1–4 3PT), 16 rebounds, 5 assists in 35 minutes

A weird game for Karl-Anthony. He had double-digit rebounds in the first half, but zero points. After intermission, he scraped together 11 points with much more aggressive drives to the rim. As New York rallied in the fourth, he banked in a short fadeaway, grabbed three defensive boards, and found Landry Shamet for the Knicks' final basket. His back up, Andre Drummond, grabbed nine boards in his 13 minutes, but dividing the workload between the pair is unsustainable. James Wiseman is glued to the bench. Leon, what's your move?

19 points (6–16 FG, 4–11 3PT, 3–6 FT), 2 rebounds, 6 assists in 33.5 minutes

Four threes and six assists are dandy, but missing half his free-throws and ten field goal attempts had us a little worried. He's been mostly automatic since coming to New York, so forgive us for jumping to conclusions like: maybe he should spend less time doing extracurricular activities and more in the gym? He started off the game great, making 3-of-4 from deep, but then disappeared for a while.

7 points (3–5 FG, 1–2 3PT) in 11 minutes (+17)

Some guys can do a lot in 11 minutes. Clarkson squeezed seven points into his sliver of time (entirely in the second half) and was still a +17. As the fourth quarter started, a game that had been a nuisance all night was finally starting to break open. Jordan blocked Nolan Traoré's layup and hit a transition three seven seconds later to push the lead to 10. He added a floater at 4:00 to keep the momentum going. He loses a point for collecting two fouls and a technical foul in the span of a minute during the third period, which didn't help matters while the team was flailing.

After the game, coach Mike Brown offered high praise for the vet. "I've been around some good teammates when it comes to players. He's gotta be the best. He's the definition of ultimate pro," he said. "You have a veteran that's been there, done that, and he's willing to accept the role that most guys wouldn't accept […]. And he's willing to accept it… and still try to uplift everybody and connect everybody while he's going through what he's going through. That's the ultimate team player."

6 points (2–4 FG, 2–4 3PT), 1 rebound, 2 assists in 16.5 minutes

Deuce hit two of four from deep and added a couple of assists. A decent shift on a night when the Knicks could've used a few more of those. Two steals is great. Four shots in almost 17 minutes seems insufficient, however.

6 points (1–7 FG, 0–2 3PT, 4–4 FT), 2 rebounds in 16 minutes (−11)

Hoo boy. Bridges made one shot from the floor and did little on defense to make up for it. The Knicks lost his 16 minutes by 11 points, the worst mark in the rotation. At least he got to the foul line for the first time after six games.

It's perhaps too soon to sound the panic alarm, but he has shot 16 of 52 for the season. Mike Brown has taken notice. He ended Kal's night after just a few minutes into the third quarter, and when he sent the starters back at the end of the game, Shamet took Bridges' spot.

The Knicks got away with three quarters of sleepwalking because Shamet kept them close and Hart, OG, and the bench gave them life in the fourth. Next up are the Bulls on Wednesday. In their first meeting of the season, last week, the Knicks won but needed 48 points from the bench to bail them out. Do we see a trend emerging?

#2757. Kevin Durant calls out reporter in uncomfortable exchange: ‘Why does that matter?’

A testy Kevin Durant got into a verbal exchange with a reporter after the Rockets' 109-93 win over the Celtics on Monday night, as the NBA superstar took issue with a question about his limited role in the offense.

Durant had just 12 points on 5-of-6 shooting from the field, while pulling down just three rebounds and recording four assists. When asked about what happened with his limited number of shots, Durant became defensive and the exchange became heated.

"Why does that matter to you? We won the game," Durant said. "Why are you singling me out? [Jabari Smith Jr.] had 30 last game and only had 14 tonight. Are you going to talk about his variety?

Rockets forward Kevin Durant (7) looks up during the third quarter against the Boston Celtics. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

​​Durant didn't let up as the reporter tried to better explain the question.

"What does that have to do with anything?," the Rockets star said. "Are you just watching me all game? Do you have the solo cam? We won the basketball game, bro."

Monday marked the fourth time in the last eight Rockets games that Durant attempted 10 or fewer shots and his 12 points were a season low.

Durant has been historically one of the best scorers in NBA history, sitting fifth on the all-time scoring list.

Eventually, Durant did answer about how his on-court presence affects what his teammates can do.

"My presence on the court, regardless, is opening it up for everybody as soon as I step on the court. They don't have to give me the ball. They're going to double-team. They're going to shade guys to me. Even when I don't have the ball," Durant said. "My presence alone is impactful to the game of basketball. I don't have to shoot. I don't have to dribble. Just me being out there is going to cause a reaction from the defense. My teammates are taking advantage of it."

Rockets' Alperen Sengun, right, blocks Celtics guard Baylor Scheierman, center, as forward Kevin Durant drives. AP Photo/Michael Wyke

Durant is averaging 20.8 points per game this season, and the Rockets are tied with the 76ers for the best record in the NBA at 7-1.

#2755. Cleveland Browns: Run defense is scary. And not in a good way.

The Cleveland Browns faced some questions on the defensive side of the ball heading into the 2026 season.

The biggest concern was how Mike Rutenberg would handle being a defensive coordinator for the first time, especially since he was taking over for the highly regarded Jim Schwartz, who had been in charge of Cleveland's defense for the past three seasons.

Outside of a Week 1 disaster against Jacksonville, the defense has been one of the consistent bright spots in Cleveland's 5-3 start.

One area that has been an issue, however, is the run defense, which has been up and down through eight games.

When the run defense has been good, it's been pretty good, as the Browns held Carolina, Baltimore, and Pittsburgh (in the first meeting) all under 100 rushing yards.

But when it has been bad, it has been bad and steadily getting worse. Cleveland allowed an average of 132.2 rushing yards to Jacksonville, Tampa Bay, and the New York Jets. But in the last two games, Tennessee rushed for 141 yards, while the Steelers piled up 200 rushing yards in Sunday's rematch.

What's worse, after allowing just one rushing touchdown in the first six games, the Browns have allowed a total of six in just the past two games.

So what is going on? Is it a lack of effort? Are opposing offenses figuring out the defense? Or is the defense wearing down from having to carry so much of the burden while the offense has struggled?

According to head coach Todd Monken, it is a combination of the last two items:

"I don't see it as a mindset. Our guys play their rear ends off – they're physical. Every game's different, every week is different. We've got to play better on the road. We've got to play from in front. That will give us a chance to play the run better and force them to make calls. But there are a number of things we're going to have to do to change, because we're going to continue to see those types of runs, those schematic runs against the attack style, and where we go from here.

"We weren't at our best the last couple of weeks in stopping the run. All right, now what? What are we going to do to stop the run? What are we going to do to put ourselves in a position where we're always trying to make a team one-dimensional, no doubt, but where we can mitigate those long plays."

It is worth pointing out that, despite the hand-wringing over Schwartz leaving the team, the Browns have been good at stopping the run for the better part of the past two decades.

According to numbers pulled by Chris Easterling at The Beacon Journal, the Browns are currently ranked No. 24 against the run as they are allowing 126.9 yards per game. Only once since 1999 have the Browns allowed less than 100 yards per game in a season, and that came in the winless campaign of 2017.

Even under the great and mighty Schwartz, the Browns struggled, as in his three seasons the defense allowed 105.5 rushing yards per game (2023), 129.7 in 2024, and 116.4 in 2025.

It doesn't seem as if the offense is in any hurry to put a full game together, so the defense is going to have to tighten up against the run if there is any hope of not having the season spiral away.

This week will be a good test to see if the coaches can make any meaningful changes as the Browns face a New Orleans team that is No. 28 in rushing yards (86.0), No. 30 in yards per carry (3.52), and just fired its offensive run coordinator.

If the Browns can't keep the Saints under control on the ground, there is no telling how bad things may get the rest of the way.

#2754. Bill Simmons compares ‘insufferable’ Luka Doncic to Gordon Ramsay in latest rant

Luka Doncic is enjoying a historic start to the 2026-27 NBA season, but Bill Simmons isn't happy with one aspect of his game.

Luka Doncic and the Los Angeles Lakers have started the season winning six out of their first eight games. The Slovenian superstar has been playing on an unbelievable level, the numbers he is putting up are spectacular.

Through seven games, Luka is averaging 38.3 points, 8.0 rebounds, and 8.1 assists per game. He is doing this while shooting 50 percent from the field, 43 percent from three-point range, and 89 percent from the free throw line. Despite all this, Bill Simmons thinks the Lakers star is 'worse than ever'.

One of the big criticisms that Doncic has faced in his career is his approach with the referees. In the past, he has received many a technical foul for complaining too much or doing it in a disrespectful manner. Luka has been called out for this numerous times, and spoken about wanting to improve.

It seems that Simmons thinks that not only has Doncic not improved, he is apparently at his worst at the moment. He said as much during one of his latest podcasts, giving an unexpected analogy.

"I think the guy is brilliant, I think he's one of the best offensive players I've ever seen in my life," Simmons said. "And it's not a new take to say that his behaviour during games has been a little insufferable. I think this is the most insufferable he's ever been.

"I can't believe how he behaves for 2.5 hours at these games and I don't know one person who enjoys it. To me, it's like when you see those commercials of Gordon Ramsay. Like, these reality chefs who are just screaming at everybody in the kitchen and you're like, 'Man it would s*** to work for that guy.'

"That's Luka now in every game, he's just unhappy constantly with everybody. And if somebody talks s*** to him on the other team, now he's got to focus his rage on this… I'm just saying… I don't want to put him on the Big TV because I don't enjoy the process of watching him get those stats."

Many analysts have said similar things about Doncic in the past; Simmons certainly isn't sharing a take which is unheard of. However, to do so at a point when the LA superstar's game is speaking volumes more than any of his antics is a bit bizarre.

#2752. Dumont family takes controlling ownership of Mavericks after purchasing shares from Miriam Adelson

Sivan and Patrick Dumont are now the controlling owners of the Dallas Mavericks.

The Dumonts purchased Miriam Adelson's shares of the Mavericks last month, according to ESPN's Tim MacMahon, which made them the controlling owners of the team with a 69% ownership share.

Though she has sold her shares to the Dumonts, Adelson is still very much connected to the Mavericks. Patrick Dumont, who has served as the Mavericks' governor since the families purchased the team from Mark Cuban in 2023, is Adelson's son-in-law. Sivan is Adelson's daughter.

"It's really about long-term investment in the city of Dallas," Dumont told ESPN on Tuesday. "The Dumont family is really committed to building what we hope is the best arena in the NBA, a really significant investment in the city that will help create the new 40-year home for the Dallas Mavericks, a great experience for our fans and inspire our team to win championships. That's really what we're focused on. We had the opportunity, and we're really excited about it."

Cuban sold the Mavericks to Adelson at a $3.5 billion valuation in 2023, though he still maintains a 27% share of the team. He's since admitted that he regretted selling the Mavericks to Adelson and Dumont in the first place, especially after the Dumonts and Adelson pushed him out of the team's basketball and business operations side. Cuban's comments came in the wake of the Luka Doncic trade, which drew intense backlash from Mavericks fans and resulted in an overhaul of the team.

Dumont, who is the Las Vegas Sands CEO, can purchase another 20% share of the team from Cuban in the future at the same valuation, too, something he said he plans to do.

"I just have to tell you, I love Mark. He's been a great family friend for a really long time," Dumont said. "He's just a super passionate Mavs fan and he's frustrated. I respect it. We're going to hopefully do him proud. That's my goal — to make all of our Mavs fans super proud about the success of the team and win a championship."

The Mavericks are expected to open a new arena at the Valley View Mall site in north Dallas in the coming years, and hope to have a new business office and practice facility to go with it. Dumont has overseen construction of the Venetian Arena in Macao, which is home to the NBA's preseason games in the country.

The Mavericks have played at the American Airlines Center since 2001, and hope to move into the new arena in time for the 2031-32 campaign.

"I think what we bring to Dallas is going to be world class," Dumont said. "My goal is to make the best arena in the NBA. It starts with the court and expands outwards, and I want to make sure the fans have the best experience possible, because we owe it to them."

The Mavericks currently hold a 4-3 record entering a matchup with the San Antonio Spurs on Wednesday night. They've not been to the playoffs since their trip to the NBA Finals with Doncic in 2024, and went just 26-56 last season.

#2750. UNM football is all about their business on the field as national buzz grows

ALBUQUERQUE, N.M. (KRQE) – It's back to the road for the 22nd-ranked University of New Mexico Lobos football team. The Lobos are in a short week, playing the Nevada Wolfpack in Reno on Friday. The Lobos head into the game with the top spot in the Mountain West, a defense that leads the FBS in multiple categories and the glare of the national spotlight. After the Lobos received their first football AP ranking in program history, national stories followed, the latest by the New York Times.

UNM head coach Jason Eck is making sure his team is grounded as the Lobos continue to handle their business on the field. "I'm happy for our fans," said Eck. "I think this is a great thing for our fans; hopefully it gets them excited about it, gets them, you know, thinking about football more. That part of it is great, but, again, for our team, we can't dwell on it because, if anything, Coach Choate's a good big game coach. I'm sure nothing more they'd like is to put a little feather of beating a ranked team.

Being ranked this week means nothing if you lose this week and get kicked out of the rankings. So, we got to take care of our business and have great preparation, and go play our best football.

The players have received the message well, and while they are thankful for the recognition, they are cautious about making too big of a deal of it. "I say it's rat poison," said UNM safety Tavian Combs. "The season's not over, a little bit over halfway through the season. Obviously, you got to be grateful because we did build it, but there's still a lot more to be done, a lot more to be accomplished. So, take it with a grain of salt. Be happy about it, but understand that humility is only a week away. If you don't come and prepare the right way that week, you could get humbled really fast."

The Lobos are 7-1 overall and 4-0 in the Mountain West. Nevada is 3-5 overall and 2-2 in league play. Game time is Friday at 7 p.m. Mountain Time on CBS Sports Network.

#2749. Bo Nix has 15 game-winning drives, most ever for a QB in his first three seasons

The Broncos trailed the Chiefs 24-13 with 10 minutes left on Sunday, but anyone who thought the Broncos were out of it hasn’t been watching Bo Nix.

Nix led Denver’s offense to two touchdowns and a two-point conversion in the fourth quarter of a 28-24 win, marking Nix’s 15th career game-winning drive in the fourth quarter or overtime. No quarterback in NFL history has as many game-winning drives in his first three seasons as Nix.

Dak Prescott, who had 14 game-winning drives in his first three seasons, had the previous record. Nix has surpassed Prescott and isn’t even halfway through his third season.

A game-winning drive is defined as an offensive scoring drive in the fourth quarter or overtime that puts a team ahead for the final time. Nix had three of them as a rookie in 2024, an NFL-high seven of them last year, and has an NFL-high five of them already this year.

The all-time leader in game-winning drives is Tom Brady, who had 58 of them. It would take Nix many years to catch Brady, but being more than a quarter of the way there through two and a half seasons is an impressive achievement.

"He lit a fire in all of us, and we had to respond," Curry said of Green.

#2747. “Did I do well, huh?” – Can Uzun responds to Hütter's criticism with strong display against HSV

Eintracht Frankfurt coach Adi Hütter caught the eye with his criticism of Can Uzun a little over a week ago, urging the 20-year-old to embrace the team game and hinting at the need to improve his defensive work rate.

Uzun has since delivered a strong response on the pitch, most recently playing a key part in Eintracht Frankfurt's 3-1 win over Hamburger SV on Sunday.

Eintracht's third goal in that game even came after Uzun won back possession and set up goalscorer Younes Ebnoutalib.

"Did I do well, huh?" Uzun joked after the match in an interview with DAZN.

"When winning the ball back leads to a goal like that, it's perfect."

The 20-year-old went on to say that he knows his coach's criticism comes from a good place.

"I'm someone who gets criticised quite often anyway, whether it's from the fans or my coach. I know he only wants to make me better."

Hütter himself also made that point clear.

"It's always about performances," the Austrian coach explained.

"I want him to become a better player. When he plays like that, the actions follow. Then he plays those through balls and wins his duels."

#2746. Chiefs-Broncos racks up 28.175 million for CBS

To no surprise, the top late-afternoon game from Week 8 of the 2026 NFL season generated very big numbers.

According to CBS, the Chiefs-Broncos game racked up 28.175 million viewers.

It was, per CBS, the most-watched Week 8 game on record.

The game was competitive and exciting until the very end, with the Broncos erasing a 24-13 fourth-quarter deficit before keeping the Chiefs from turning a first-and-goal from the four into a game-winning score, with the fourth-down play being the last one of the game.

It will undoubtedly be the most-watched Week 8 game, given the NFL's decision to leave the game between the Eagles and Commanders in prime time, despite multiple viable flex options.

#2745. Region semifinal pairings finalized for Watertown, Aberdeen area teams

Four teams in each area Class A and B region volleyball tournaments are slated to play for berths in the upcoming SoDak 16 state-qualifying matches.

Region tournament play opened Monday and Tuesday for area Class A and B teams. Region semifinals are slated for Thurday with the winners advancing to SoDak 16 matches on Tuesday, Nov. 10.

SoDak 16 winners will qualify for the state tournament Nov. 19-21 at Sioux Falls.

Here's a breakdown of what's happened so far in the area regions:

GROTON — Second-seeded Groton Area, third-seeded Britton-Hecla and fourth-seeded Webster Area all recorded quarterfinal wins on Tuesday.

Groton Area beat seventh-seeded Sisseton 3-0 (25-11, 25-10 and 25-17); Britton-Hecla topped sixth-seeded Redfield 3-1 (25-13, 25-17, 22-25 and 25-16); and Webster Area downed Milbank 3-0 (25-22, 25-18 and 25-20).

Sisseton finished 4-21, Redfield 6-20 and Milbank Area 9-17.

Semifinal games on Thursday include Webster Area (13-11) at top-seeded Aberdeen Roncalli (21-5) and Britton-Hecla (17-6) at Groton Area (19-13).

Second-seeded Hamlin, third-seeded Sioux Valley and fourth-seeded Elkton-Lake Benton all notched quarterfinal wins on their home courts on Tuesday.

Hamlin turned back seventh-seeded Florence-Henry 3-0 (25-12, 25-13 and 25-8), Sioux Valley blanked sixth-seeded Great Plains Lutheran 3-0 (25-9, 25-11 and 25-20) and Elkton-Lake Benton downed fifth-seeded Deuel 3-1 (22-25, 25-21, 25-14 and 25-21).

Great Plains Lutheran ended its season at 10-17, Deuel 10-14 and Florence-Henry 4-22.

Hamlin moved into the semifinals by getting six aces and seven kills from Mady Jutting, four aces and 12 digs from Jayci Trowbridge, 22 assists and seven digs from Issie Steffensen, nine kills from Addie Jensen, five kills from Kayde Nebel, 11 digs from Paxton Neuendorf and three blocks from Lexa Thue.

Elkton-Lake Benton (9-16) visits top-seeded Clark-Willow Lake (22-1) at 7 p.m. in Willow Lake and Sioux Valley (21-7) plays Hamlin (20-3) at 7 p.m. at the Hamlin Education Center in semifinal games on Thursday.

FINAL STATE VOLLEYBALL RANKINGS: Harrisburg, Sioux Falls Christian & Warner remain No. 1 teams in poll

Third-seeded Mobridge-Pollock and fourth-seeded Chamberlain each advanced with wins on Tuesday. Mobridge-Pollock won 3-0 (25-16, 25-11 and 25-11) over sixth-seeded Cheyenne-Eagle Butte in a match played at Mobridge and Chamberlain earned a 3-1 (25-22, 25-18, 18-25 and 25-14) win over fifth-seeded Stanley County in match played at Chamberlain.

In semifinal games on Thursday, Chamberlain (9-17) visits top-seeded Winner (19-9) and Mobridge-Pollock (18-15) visits No. 2 Platte-Geddes (20-10).

The tourney opened on Monday with ninth-seeded Wilmot beating eighth-seeded Tiospa Zina 3-0 (25-13, 25-10 and 25-17) at Agency Village and seventh-seeded Waverly-South Shore posting a 3-1 (25-17, 23-25, 25-16 and 28-26) win over 10th-seeded Leola-Frederick Area. Tiospa Zina closed its season at 8-21 and LFA 4-17.

In quarterfinals on Tuesday, top-seeded Warner defeated Wilmot 3-0 (25-4, 25-6 and 25-2) at Warner; fifth-seeded Hitchcock-Tulare won 3-1 (25-18, 26-25, 22-25 and 25-18) at fourth-seeded Langford Area; second-seeded Northwestern rolled past Waverly-South Shore 3-0 (25-15, 25-8 and 25-9) and third-seeded Aberdeen Christian prevailed over sixth-seeded Waubay-Summit 3-0 (25-20, 25-23 and 25-17).

Wilmot finished the season 6-17, Waverly-South Shore 12-17, Langford Area 15-14 and Waubay-Summit 10-15.

Tatumn Hanson (five aces and 18 assists), Keira Steger (six aces and seven digs), Olivia Marcuson (eight kills), Miah Leidholt and Kya Townsend (six kills each), Claire Bakeberg (nine assists) and Jaycee Jung (eight digs) powered Warner. Cali Weber notched 24 digs, Natalie Remily nine blocks and Camria Cameron five blocks for Wilmot.

Northwestern moved on with help from Lilly Haven, nine aces and 10 digs; Sophia Troske, six aces; Elexis Kramp, nine kills; Breelyn Satter, eight kills; Tayler Vetter, seven kills and 12 digs; Lila Johnson, 25 assists; and Kylee Henjum, three block assists. Jaina Johnson recorded five kills and 10 digs and Marlie Riter nine assists for Waverly-South Shore.

Emmerie Arthurs colllected 22 kills and 19 digs, Keilee Schone 10 kills, Brynn Binger and Laina Puffer 20 assists each, Olivia Knox 30 digs, Tess Bixler five blocks and Rylee Knock six kills and four blocks for Hitchcock-Tulare.

In semifinal matches at 6 p.m. on Thursday, Hitchcock-Tulare (17-14) visits Warner (31-3) and Aberdeen Christian (21-9) plays at Northwestern (31-5).

Eighth-seeded De Smet swept ninth-seeded Oldham-Ramona-Rutland 3-0 (25-16, 25-18 and 26-24) and seventh-seeded Iroquois-Lake Preston rallied past 10th-seeded James Valley Christian 3-1 (19-25, 25-11, 25-17 and 26-24) on their own home courts in games on Monday. ORR ended the season 6-20 and JVC 7-18.

De Smet leaders included Charli McCune, four aces, six kills, 22 digs and 14 blocks; Gemma Luethmers 13 kills and seven blocks; Lily Cleveland six kills; Vada Albrecht 20 assists; Megan Albrecht, 43 digs and Ava Poppinga 22 digs. Jordy Reagle had six kills, Logyn Reagle nine asssits, Amelia Malisch 12 digs and Brooklyn Hyland nine digs for ORR.

On Tuesday at Wolsey-Wessington, top-seeded Wolsey-Wessington beat De Smet 3-0 (25-4, 25-7 and 25-13) and fourth-seeded Castlewood turned back fifth-seeded Arlington 3-0 (25-12, 25-14 and 25-22) in quarterfinal games. De Smet closed its season 7-21 and Arlington 14-16.

In other quarterfinal games at Estelline, second-seeded Estelline-Hendricks won 3-0 (25-15 25-21 and 25-22) over Iroquois-Lake Preston and third-seeded Deubrook Area also won 3-0 (25-13, 25-7 and 25-14) over sixth-seeded Wessington Springs. ILP finished the season at 9-23 and Wessington Springs 13-17.

Estelline-Hendricks was led by Elyse Wegner, three aces and eight kills; Presly Bury, seven aces, 15 assists and eight digs; Kori Saathoff, nine kills; Leandra Harms six kills and three solo blocks; Cambry Bury, 18 assists; Kimmy Schaefer 17 digs and Hilary Ramirez nine digs. Khloe Olson tallied six kills, Alise Hope five kills, Sydney Bonomi 20 assists and 12 digs and Belle Bonomi 13 digs for ILP.

In semifinal games on Thursday, Castlewood (16-11) visits Wolsey-Wessington (25-5) and Deubrook Area (22-7) plays Estelline-Henricks (22-5) in Estelline. Both games are at 7 p.m.

VOLLEYBALL: Watertown, Aberdeen Central squads each chasing state AA tourney berths

The tourney opened Monday with eighth-seeded Sunshine Bible Academy, seventh-seeded Gettysburg and sixth-seeded North Central all notching wins on their own home courts. SBA beat ninth-seeded Tiospaye Topa 3-0 (25-8, 25-17 and 25-13), Gettysburg swept 10th-seeded Sully Buttes 3-0 (25-15, 25-17 and 25-12) and North Central also winning 3-0 (25-6, 25-5 and 25-6) over 11th-seeded Wakpala. Tiospaye Topa ended its season at 9-20, Sully Buttes 3-24 and Wakpala 3-23.

Kaidence Bulgin tallied eight kills, Ava Gonska seven aces, Ashton Heyne seven assists and eight aces, Tylee Vander Vorst six kills and six aces and Camryn Witlock seven assists for North Central.

In quarterfinal games on Tuesday, top-seeded Miller won 3-0 (25-11, 25-14 and 25-19) over SBA in Miller; fifth-seeded Herreid-Selby Area beat fourth-seeded Ipswich 3-0 (25-23, 25-15 and 25-15) at Ipswich; second-seeded Faulkton Area blanked Gettysburg 3-0 (25-13, 25-21 and 25-19) in Faulkton and third-seeded Highmore-Harrold recorded a 3-1 (25-15, 25-18, 21-25 and 25-18) win over North Central at Highmore. SBA closed the season 11-17, Ipswich 16-12, Gettysburg 13-14 and North Central 15-15.

Emma Pugh's seven aces, nine kills and 11 digs sparked Miller, which also received four aces from Kalley Teason, 11 kills and 10 digs from Hailey Werdel, six kills and 11 digs from Olivia Nye and 12 digs from Autumn Flor. Ciera Mickelson had eight aces and five blocks, Sidney Stoddard six blocks and Dakota Boersma six digs for SBA.

Claire Cotton made two aces and 30 assists, Anika Mueller 11 kills, Austina Sorensen 25 digs and Brooklyn Mueller 12 digs for Faulkton Area. Brynlie Ahlemeier tallied five kills, four block assists and 15 digs for Gettysburg. Layla Cronin added five kills, Finlee Heien 14 assists and Taylor Long 20 digs.

Kaydence Hobert notched six kills, Jada Baloun five kills and nine digs, Lainee Ping 14 digs and Callie Chavez six assits to lead Highmore-Harrold. Vander Vorst had 13 kills and 14 digs, Lindsey Hoerner seven kills and 17 digs, Peyton Schnabel eight kills, Heyne 16 assists and nine digs, Witlock seven assists and nine digs and Kelsey Sowards 11 digs for North Central.

Semifinal games Thursday included HSA (14-17) at Miller (20-9) and Highmore-Harrold (18-10) at Faulkton Area (19-11).

Watertown and Aberdeen Central each closed out their regular season on Tuesday. The Arrows lost at No. 2 Class AA Brandon Valley 3-0 (25-22, 25-17 and 25-22) and Aberdeen Central won 3-0 (25-19, 25-16 and 25-22) at home against Sioux Falls Roosevelt.

Watertown (18-9) and Aberdeen Central (16-10) are each in position to host SoDak 16 state-qualifying games on Thursday, Nov. 12. Those pairings are expected to be finalized this weekend.
